Corollary 3.2: RESTATEMENT. Its layer generating function and adjacent-layer formula are respectively the generating transform and first difference of Proposition 3.1’s exact identity; the proof explicitly obtains them by those two algebraic operations.

Lemma 3.3: RESTATEMENT. It unpacks the already stated identities \(U_s=(1-B_s)^{-1}\) and “\(u_j\) is the total generator weight” into word-sum and convolution-probability notation.

Lemma 4.2: RESTATEMENT. Absolute convergence of \(b_C\), \(b_C=8\), the local denominator asymptotic, the cost-tail asymptotic, regular variation, and \(K_C\) were all already stated in Theorem 4.1 and its former proof; the new lemma supplies a more explicit summation argument.

Lemma 4.3: RESTATEMENT. The previous proof already displayed the same general second-order renewal asymptotic with the same hypotheses; the new lemma improves the citation locator and derives the coefficient from the equilibrium tail using discrete Karamata summation.

Proposition 4.4: RESTATEMENT. The paper already asserted normalization, aperiodicity from costs \(3,5\), finite mean, regular variation, and applicability of the renewal theorem; the new proof makes those checks explicit and newly justifies \(1<\alpha<2\).

Lemma 4.5: NEW. It states and substantially proves a generic one-sided stable-domain lemma for every nonnegative \(X\), rather than only the paper’s particular \(C\); this is new within the manuscript, although it is a standard Feller result, not new research mathematics.

Corollary 4.6: RESTATEMENT. Its first expansion is exactly Theorem 4.1 after replacing \(Z_m^R\) by its defining sum, and its second expansion is obtained solely by factoring out \(2m/\mu_C\).

Remark 4.7: NEW. The numerical value of \(\sigma_0\), the value of \(K_C\), and the four finite-\(Q\) checks were not elsewhere in the paper; they are new consistency data, not a new analytical conclusion.

2. CITED-VS-PROVED

The core second-order renewal theorem is still a black box. The paper cites Omey–Van Gulck for
\[
v_j-\frac1\mu\sim\frac{\overline F^{\rm e}(j)}\mu.
\]
It does not prove that renewal theorem. What it now proves in-house is the conversion
\[
\overline F(j)\sim Kj^{-\alpha}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\overline F^{\rm e}(j)\sim\frac{K}{\mu(\alpha-1)}j^{1-\alpha},
\]
and hence the displayed coefficient \(K/\{\mu^2(\alpha-1)\}\).

For the critical letter law it now explicitly verifies: total mass one from \(B_{\sigma_0}(1)=1\); positive-integer support; span one from positive masses at \(3\) and \(5\); finite mean; \(2<\sigma_0<3\), hence \(1<\alpha<2\); and the regularly varying tail with the exact constant \(K_C\). The finite-mean verification still relies on the cited Panov–Liehl fixed-denominator estimate, although the summability deduction from that estimate is shown.

Feller is still cited, but the stable step is no longer merely discharged by citation. The paper proves the positive and negative tail balance, \(n\Pr\{X>a_nx\}\to x^{-\alpha}\) and zero negative tail; the normalization \(n\Pr\{X>a_n\}\to1\); convergence of the tail measures; the truncated second-moment and large-jump first-moment bounds; convergence of the compensated characteristic exponent; and the logarithmic expansion showing that centering by \(n\mu\) gives the claimed stable law. Standard regular-variation integration and vague-convergence facts are invoked tersely, but Feller is no longer carrying the whole specialized conclusion.

3. CORRECTNESS SPOT CHECK

(a) Pass. The defining equation is
\[
\frac{\zeta(\sigma_0-1)}{\zeta(\sigma_0)}=2,
\]
so \(b_C=2(2)^2=8\). Numerically, \(\sigma_0=2.478750785733960\ldots\), and both formulas give \(K_C=15.07798619025016\ldots\).

(b) Pass. Since costs \(3\) and \(5\) both occur with positive mass, the gcd of the whole support divides \(\gcd(3,5)=1\), so the lattice span is one. Variable-length sums of odd increments cause no parity obstruction; nonnegative sums of \(3\) and \(5\) contain every integer from \(8\) onward.

(c) Pass. With \(\Pr\{C>x\}\sim K_Cx^{-\alpha}\) and \(a_n=(K_Cn)^{1/\alpha}\),
\[
n\Pr\{C>a_n\}\sim nK_Ca_n^{-\alpha}
=\frac{nK_C}{K_Cn}=1.
\]
The explicit \(K_C=2^{\sigma_0+2}/(\sigma_0-1)\) also follows correctly from \(\alpha=\sigma_0-1\) and \(b_C=8\).

There is one separate minor error: with
\[
p_j^{\rm e}=\frac{\overline F(j-1)}{\mu},
\]
the exact identity should be
\[
\sum_{k>j}p_k^{\rm e}
=\frac1\mu\sum_{r\ge j}\overline F(r),
\]
not \(\mu^{-1}\sum_{r>j}\overline F(r)\) as printed. The omitted term is lower order, so the Karamata asymptotic and final renewal coefficient remain unchanged.

4. FILLER SCAN

Pure throat-clearing:
“The proof is separated into the arithmetic tail calculation, the lattice renewal input, and the one-sided stable criterion. This also fixes the normalizations at each transfer.”

Corollary 4.6’s proof openly confirms that it is repackaging:
“The interval in [the displayed formula] is exactly \(\mathcal I_m\), so the left side is \(Z_m^R(-\sigma_0)\). Equation [the theorem’s formula] proves the first expansion. Factoring out \(2m/\mu_C\) gives [the relative formula].”

The new conclusion also repeats Theorem 4.1 and the introduction:
“The transfer has two distinct probabilistic outputs. For independent critical generator letters, the explicit scale … gives a centered spectrally positive stable limit. For the deterministic classical partition function, the exact orbit identity converts the same tail into …”

Those passages are genuine repetition. By contrast, the new paragraph listing limitations, no local limit theorem, no convergence rate, critical exponent only, and no universality claim, adds useful scope control rather than filler.

5. VERDICT

The eight pages do not introduce a new paper-level theorem beyond what Theorem 4.1 already claimed. Most of the new numbered results are reorganizations of existing statements. Nevertheless, the development is not merely padding: the explicit tail summation, complete renewal-hypothesis audit, derivation of the second-order coefficient, proof of \(2<\sigma_0<3\), and characteristic-function derivation of the stable normalization are real proof content that materially improves referee auditability. Section 4 is now self-contained at the level of applying the external renewal theorem and is essentially self-contained for the stable specialization, but it is not literally standalone because the decisive Omey–Van Gulck renewal asymptotic and the Panov–Liehl mean estimate remain external inputs. A TAMS referee would likely regard the application as adequately documented, provided the precise Omey–Van Gulck citation is correct, while still recognizing that the core second-order renewal theorem has not been proved in the paper.

I captured and read the complete requested `git diff HEAD -- ...` before that happened. The two verification scripts were untracked at that point, so they were not part of the requested diff; the concurrent commit subsequently included them. I did not run `latexmk`.
